Monday, October 5, 2026

arXiv:2610.03472 §

Three-loop rapidity anomalous dimension for jet-veto cross sections

Samuel Abreu et al.

This paper supplies the last missing ingredient for N³LL resummation of the colour-singlet \(p_T^{\rm jet}<p_T^{\rm veto}\) cross section. The authors compute \(\gamma_\nu^{(3)}(R^2)\) from the three-loop SCET soft function, using the exponential rapidity regulator, for anti-\(k_t\), C/A and \(k_t\). The two-loop beam and soft functions, four-loop cusp and three-loop non-cusp anomalous dimensions were already known.

Method. The key device is a non-physical “Reference Simplified Variable” (RSV). It applies the veto to the total \(p_t\) of each correlated cluster in the non-abelian-exponentiation decomposition, so it is \(R\)-independent and factorises cluster by cluster.

  • \(\ln\mathcal{S}_{\rm RSV}\) is a Bessel-kernel transform of the TMD soft function. This gives \(\gamma_{\nu,\rm RSV}=\gamma_{\nu,\perp}+(\alpha_s/4\pi)^3\,\tfrac{32}{3}C_R b_0^2\zeta_3\) at three loops.
  • The remainder \(\Delta\mathcal{S}\) vanishes for single emission, so the two-loop single-real amplitude is never needed. Its singularity structure also drops by two orders: at three loops it has only one-loop-type poles.
  • The remainder splits into III, CCI and CCC pieces, which scale at small \(R\) as \(R^4\), \(R^2\ln R^2\) and \(\ln^2R^2\) respectively. Integrations are done semi-numerically with Cuba, cross-checked with VegasFlow.

Results. Three loops is the first order at which the result depends on the clustering algorithm, but the spread between the three algorithms is very mild. For \(n_f=5\), \(C_R=C_A\), the three-loop coefficients are:

\(R\) Three-loop coefficient Two-loop coefficient (for comparison)
0.1 77165 ± 0.36‰ 2174.69
0.4 28537 ± 0.38‰ 1135.60
0.8 6645 ± 0.97‰ 498.59

At \(p_T^{\rm veto}=30\) GeV the three-loop term is a 15–40% correction to \(\gamma_\nu\), largest at small \(R\).

Checks and caveats. - IR poles cancel numerically in all colour structures; for \(C_RC_AT_Rn_f\) this requires combining quark and gluon final states. - The known LL \(\ln^2R^2\) coefficient is reproduced. - The LL small-\(R\) approximation is poor even at very small \(R\); a fit including the NLL \(\ln R^2\) term is good up to \(R\sim0.4\), where it overshoots by ~10%. This implies a large NLL small-\(R\) coefficient. - Results are numerical rather than analytic. - Coherence-violating effects are noted but enter only at five loops beyond leading \(N_c\). - The provided text truncates before the phenomenological impact discussion.

The RSV subtraction strategy is pitched as reusable for other clustering-dependent three-loop soft functions.

arXiv:2610.03489 §

CMS search for TT̅→(ϕt)(ϕt), ϕ→γγ, boosted all-hadronic tops

CMS Collaboration

This is a substructure application rather than a methods paper. It uses 138 fb⁻¹ at 13 TeV.

Selection. - Two AK8 jets with \(p_T>350\) GeV and soft-drop mass (\(\beta=0\), \(z_{\rm cut}=0.1\)) \(m_{\rm SD}>50\) GeV, with \(|\Delta\eta|<1.6\). - Both jets ParticleNet top-tagged at the 0.5% light-jet mistag working point (~40% top efficiency). - Two photons; the signal region requires both to pass the WP80 identification, and a control region requires both to fail it.

Background estimate and fit. Background is extrapolated from the control region with an exponential transfer function in \(m_\phi^{\rm reco}\). The fit is two-dimensional in \((m_T^{\rm reco},m_\phi^{\rm reco})\) over \(m_\phi=100\)–300 GeV and \(m_T=800\)–1800 GeV.

Results. - The largest local excess is 2.7σ (1.5σ global) at \(m_T=900\), \(m_\phi=150\) GeV. It is a single event against a predicted \(7\times10^{-6}\). - Upper limits are set on \(\sigma\,\mathcal{B}(T\to\phi t)^2\,\mathcal{B}_{\gamma\gamma}(2-\mathcal{B}_{\gamma\gamma})\).

Jet-specific systematics. The analysis is statistics-dominated. The ParticleNet uncertainty has a 1.0% impact on \(r\), and JES/JMS/JER/JMR are smaller still; FSR (3.0%) leads.

Caveats. - The second \(\phi\) is left undecayed in simulation; the authors note this may slightly underestimate signal acceptance. - Jet–diphoton pairing is correct only 50–75% of the time (~40% at the lowest \(m_T\)). - Validation is statistically limited (VR \(p\)-value 0.14).

arXiv:2610.03322 §

EOS version 1.1

Nienke C. Balz et al.

This paper is not about jets; it presumably matched on the \(e^+e^-\to\) hadrons keyword. That addition is a coupled-channel K-matrix description of \(R\) and \(D\bar D\) in the charmonium region, with no jet observables. The rest covers flavour observables, the analysis-file format, pyhf likelihood import, and nested sampling in cumulative-prior space. Skip unless the flavour or Bayesian-sampling content is of interest.

Friday, October 2, 2026

arXiv:2610.00514 §

Unified description of longitudinal and transverse parton dynamics in cold nuclear matter

Weiyao Ke

Relevance to this topic: This paper is only peripherally about jets. It concerns parton propagation in cold nuclear matter for SIDIS and Drell–Yan. The link to substructure is the final section on the one-point energy correlator (OPEC) in DIS.

What they did. Ke combines two strands of SCET_G work into one TMD-level matching coefficient, Eq. (1): - Longitudinal part. Impact-parameter-dependent medium-induced evolution M^med, building on Ke–Vitev’s RG analysis of collinear SIDIS. The coupled quark/gluon equations (2)–(3) are in the dilute limit υ ≃ α_s ρ_G L²/(4E) ≪ 1. They run over ξ² ≲ μ² ≲ min{2E/L, μ_b²}. Finite b therefore truncates the coherent-radiation window, so energy loss depends on the transverse resolution. - Transverse part. Broadening through the exponentiated Glauber cross section Σ_i(b,Y). - Fix to the collision kernel. The kernel is BFKL-evolved in rapidity, but leading-order BFKL “produces excessively strong radiative broadening.” It is tamed with a Kwieciński-type kinematical constraint (following Deak et al.) and a Kovchegov–Weigert-style running coupling.

Results. One parameter set (ρ_G = 0.5 fm⁻³, ξ² = 0.12 GeV²) reproduces the main trends of HERMES Xe/d multiplicity ratios and E866 Fe/Be and W/Be Drell–Yan ratios. The paper also gives EIC e+Pb/e+d predictions at √s = 60 GeV, Q² = 20 GeV², x_B = 0.1 and 0.4. Attenuation is stronger at x_B = 0.4, because the rest-frame parton energy E ≃ Q²/(2m_N x_B) is lower.

The jet-substructure-relevant point. Energy weighting kills the leading final-state energy-loss enhancement via the momentum sum rule. For the OPEC at √s = 141 GeV and 10 < Q < 15 GeV: - the dynamical cold-nuclear-matter correction is below 1% in the back-to-back region; - the roughly 30% nuclear modification comes from the nPDFs.

So for nuclear energy correlators, the final-state medium effect largely cancels at this order. This makes the OPEC an nPDF probe rather than a medium probe, and is worth knowing before interpreting eA energy-correlator data as jet quenching.

Caveats. - Proceedings-length text; data agreement is described only as “main trends,” with no fit quality quoted. - Vacuum nonperturbative TMD input is used, and in-nucleus hadronization is explicitly excluded. - The υ ≪ 1 limit is stretched at low-E HERMES and EIC kinematics.

Thursday, October 1, 2026

arXiv:2609.39381 §

New Observables for Resonant and Non-Resonant New Physics Searches at Colliders

Julien Touch\`eque

This is a UCLouvain PhD thesis (Touchèque, supervised by Degrande). It is only tangential to substructure. The supplied text runs through the abstract, introduction and textbook SM material in Chapter 1, and is cut off before the analysis chapters. So the jet content below rests only on the abstract and chapter outline.

The relevant part is Chapter 4, which searches for top-philic colour-singlet and colour-octet scalars in four-top final states. It covers fully hadronic, leptonic and same-sign dileptonic channels, with signals generated at NLO QCD. The scalar decays yield boosted \(t\bar t\) pairs:

  • Hadronic tops are reconstructed with ML taggers for wide hadronic jets.
  • Leptonic tops are reconstructed assuming on-shell \(W\) decays.
  • Event selection uses a new scheme built on the invariant masses of top pairs.

The abstract claims limits improved by up to an order of magnitude over a recast CMS analysis. The underlying work is published (JHEP 11 (2025) 091, plus PRD 111 (2025) 055037), so there is nothing new here for substructure beyond packaging. If boosted multi-top tagging matters to you, read the JHEP paper directly.

Chapter 3, on CP-odd SMEFT triple-product asymmetries in \(WZ\)/\(W\gamma\), is unrelated to jets.

arXiv:2609.39515 §

Probing jet quenching via the correlation of groomed jet substructure observables in Pb–Pb and pp collisions

ALICE Collaboration

Setup. ALICE reports a fully corrected 2D \((\theta_g, z_g)\) measurement, unfolded with 3D iterative Bayesian unfolding in \((p_T, z_g, \theta_g)\). The sample is:

  • Charged \(R=0.2\) anti-\(k_T\) jets with \(60\le p_{T,\rm ch\,jet}<80\) GeV.
  • pp (18 nb\(^{-1}\)) and 0–10% Pb–Pb (0.12 nb\(^{-1}\)) at 5.02 TeV.
  • Soft Drop with \(z_{\rm cut}=0.2\), \(\beta=0\); tagging fractions are 89% (pp) and 87% (Pb–Pb).
  • Projections in two bins: \(z_g\) split at 0.35, \(\theta_g\) split at 0.3.

Results.

  • Narrowing: Pb–Pb jets are narrower in both \(z_g\) bins. The appendix double ratio is flat, so the narrowing is effectively \(z_g\)-independent once the yield shift is accounted for.
  • Narrow jets (\(\theta_g<0.3\)): \(z_g\) shows no modification.
  • Wide jets (\(\theta_g>0.3\)): the Pb/pp \(z_g\) ratio has a slope (less balanced in Pb–Pb). Relative to a constant of ≈0.82, the significance is 2.7σ statistical only and 2.0σ with systematics. It is limited by pp statistics.
  • pp generators: PYTHIA and POWHEG describe the data, while Herwig has too few wide jets.
  • Pb–Pb models:
  • JEWEL overestimates the narrowing.
  • JETSCAPE describes it well but over-depletes large \(\theta_g\) for unbalanced jets.
  • The Hybrid model is insensitive to the wake. At large \(\theta_g\), unbalanced jets prefer the variant with elastic (Molière) scattering and balanced jets the variant without.

Caveats. Survival bias is not disentangled; the authors themselves point to the CMS γ-tagged \(R_g\) result. The aggressive grooming at small \(R\) kills sensitivity to medium response. Residual background mistags of 5–15% are handled via thermal non-closure.

The main advance is that this is the first correlated \((\theta_g, z_g)\) unfolding in AA. The physics signal, however, is a 2σ hint.

arXiv:2609.40224 §

In medium evolution of the energy-energy correlators

Weiyao Ke

These are Ke’s proceedings, summarising Ke–Meçaj–Vitev (JHEP 04 (2026) 155).

Framework. In SCET\(_G\) at first order in opacity, the regime is thin, dilute media with \(p_T/L\gg\alpha_s\rho_G L\sim m_D^2\). The EEC jet function receives two kinds of medium correction:

  1. Non-contact fixed-order correction. It changes character at \(\theta_{\rm LPM}=\sqrt{8\pi/(p_T L)}\) and carries a Coulomb log. It depletes the EEC below \(\theta_{\rm LPM}\) and enhances it above.
  2. Medium-induced anomalous dimension. This is a matrix \(\boldsymbol\Gamma^{\rm med}(N)\) acting in the window \(\Lambda_{\rm QCD}/p_T\ll\theta\ll\theta_{\rm LPM}\). Its strength is set by \(w_{\rm med}=4\pi\alpha_s\rho_{\rm eff}L_{\rm eff}^2/(2p_T)\). The diagonal entries grow linearly in \(N\), which motivates ratios of higher-point correlators to the EEC. Its effect is extra small-angle suppression, stronger for gluon jets.

The medium enters only through two path-integrated quantities, \(\rho_{\rm eff}\) and \(L_{\rm eff}\); coherence means the local density profile is not resolved at this order. The switch from vacuum to modified evolution occurs at \(\mu_m\sim\sqrt{p_T/L_{\rm eff}}\).

Phenomenology.

  • p–Pb: compared with preliminary ALICE minimum-bias data for \(R=0.4\) jets in charged-jet \(p_T\) bins of 20–80 GeV. The calculation gives small-angle suppression that weakens with \(p_T\), consistent with the data.
  • O–O: projections are given for O–O collisions at 5.36 TeV.

Caveats.

  • Charged jets are approximated by \(p_T^{\rm ch}\simeq\tfrac23 p_T^{\rm full}\), with no track functions.
  • The uncertainty bands vary only the matching scale, not the medium parameters.
  • Hadronization dominates at small \(\theta\), and recoil and medium response matter near \(\theta\sim R\).

Connection to the ALICE paper. Both are probing medium-modified angular structure, but in opposite regimes. ALICE uses dense central Pb–Pb with hard grooming, where survival bias dominates. Ke works in the thin-medium limit, where a modified anomalous dimension is a cleaner, calculable target. The \(p_T\) and system-size scaling of \(w_{\rm med}\) offers a falsifiable test that groomed \(z_g\) currently does not.

Wednesday, September 30, 2026

arXiv:2609.36523 §

Non-global logarithms and fiducial transverse-momentum-dependent observables in deep-inelastic scattering

Shuo Lin et al.

Lin and Zhou replace the lepton–jet imbalance with a jet-free fiducial imbalance, qT = l′_T + Σ{h∈|η|<Δη/2} p_hT. By momentum conservation this equals minus the total p_T outside the window, so the rapidity window plays the role of the jet boundary. At one loop, the jet-radius coefficients c₀(R), c₁(R) are replaced by analytic B₀(Δη) = −Δη − 2ln(1−e^{−Δη}) and B₁(Δη) = 4 atanh(e^{−Δη/2}) − 2e^{−Δη/2}. For reference, inclusive-k_T at R=1 gives c₀ = −0.253 and c₁ = 0.782.

The non-global treatment has three parts: - They derive closed-form two-loop angular coefficients I₀ and I₁ (dilogarithm expressions). - The azimuthal-average NGL U₀ is resummed at LL, large-N_c, with a BMS dipole-shower Monte Carlo using a 1 − J₀(bk_T) weight. - The cos φ harmonic NGL is kept only at O(α_s²). It is single-log, U₁⁽²⁾ ~ ᾱ_s² I₁ ln(P_T/μ_b), whereas U₀⁽²⁾ is double-log.

Headline numbers: - ⟨cos φ⟩ at q_T = 1 GeV roughly halves from Δη = 1 to Δη = 2. For HERA it goes 0.280 → 0.134 → 0.074 for Δη = 1, 2, 3. - At the EIC, |A_UT| and |A_LT| grow with Δη and peak near q_T ~ 1–1.5 GeV. - A_LT at Δη = 1 and q_T = 1 GeV is ≈0.25 at EicC versus ≈0.02 at EIC.

For this topic, the paper gives an algorithm-independent recoil-control knob with analytic boundary dependence. It is a direct comparator to the H1 ML-unfolded lepton-jet measurements, and scanning Δη within one dataset tests the global and non-global contributions together.

Caveats: - NGLs are LL and large-N_c only, and the first-harmonic NGL is not resummed. - The S_NP inputs are borrowed from Drell–Yan fits (halved). - Twist-3 evolution of T_F and g̃ is approximated by unpolarized DGLAP. - The calculation is at parton level, with no hadronization or detector acceptance effects.

arXiv:2609.36933 §

Flavor-resolved four-fermion probes at the FCC-ee Z pole

Syuhei Iguro

This paper is only tangential to jets. Jet flavor tagging is an enabling assumption, but the analysis is parton-level and has no jet definition; the author notes the ΔR_min = 0.2 cut must eventually become an IR-safe jet definition.

The physics: using Z → QQ̄ℓℓ (Q = b, c; ℓ = μ, τ) at 200 ab⁻¹, the author sets tree-level, interference-only 95% CL reaches on vector contact operators of (2.5, 2.7, 3.5, 3.6) TeV for bμ, bτ, cμ, cτ. These assume unit efficiency, and the reach scales as ε_rec^{1/4}. The main point is flavor resolution. The projected EWPO likelihood in the (C^{bτ}, C^{cτ}) plane has correlation ρ = −0.9999 and effectively constrains only C^{bτ} + 0.058 C^{cτ}. The four-body channels constrain the flat direction, giving combined reaches of Λ^{bτ} > 11.8 TeV and Λ^{cτ} > 3.2 TeV.

What matters for jets: - The optimal hardness cut is only 3–5 GeV, so this needs b/c tagging on soft jets in busy four-body topologies. The author explicitly flags this as unvalidated. - b↔c migration cannot be absorbed into a common efficiency factor.

Tuesday, September 29, 2026

arXiv:2609.31862 §

Prompting Particle Physics: Tokenized Multi-modal Foundation Models for Combinatorially Many Tasks

Nilotpal Kakati et al.

The authors tokenize seven per-jet modalities in COCOA single-jet events (88.9M train, median truth-jet p_T 46 GeV). Content features use residual VQ-VAEs and positions use 1024-bin (η, cos φ, sin φ); calorimeter cells enter as 8×8 patch images. The modalities are tracks, topoclusters, cells, HGPflow particles, truth particles, truth jet and per-cell charged fraction. One model is trained over all 1302 input→output subsets with ≤3 modalities on each side. There are two decoders: nanoHEP (GPT-2-style, autoregressive) and HEP4M (4M-style, parallel with cardinality heads).

The jet-relevant benchmark is particle flow (TK+CL→TP), scored against truth particles:

Model Jet p_T IQR Median response C2ST AUC
HGPflow 0.0754 0.986 0.902
nanoHEP-pflow 0.0733 0.978 0.691
HEP4M-pflow 0.0836 0.994 0.940

The C2ST is an event-conditioned set-transformer two-sample test. Its point is that HGPflow matches on aggregate jet metrics but its constituent cloud is easily separable from truth, which argues for particle-cloud-level validation beyond jet response.

  • Extra inputs help. Adding cells (TK+CL+CE) gives IQR 0.0704 and AUC 0.650.
  • Unseen four-input combination. TK+CL+RP+CE, never presented in training, reaches 0.0688.

Caveats: - Not substructure. The paper is about reconstructing and simulating jet constituents; no substructure observables are shown. - Multi-task gain is small. On the benchmark itself, nanoHEP-multi (0.0781) is slightly worse than nanoHEP-pflow; at matched steps, from-scratch training gives 0.0758. - Truth-jet-input row is not a fair comparison. The 0.0305 IQR uses the truth jet as an input. - Refinement fails. RP→TP does not improve on the HGPflow input. - Cell generation fails. Generated cells are fully separable from truth (AUC 0.998), and the cell tokenizer round trip alone is separable at 0.96. - HEP4M artefacts. It needs cos/sin renormalisation and under-produces high topocluster multiplicities. - Simplified setup. The detector is simplified, tracks are parameterised fast simulation, and pileup is not discussed.

arXiv:2609.32054 §

The era of charmed resonance searches at the LHC

Daniel Whiteson

This is a flavour-tagging projection. The author starts from the ATLAS 139 fb⁻¹ untagged dijet spectrum and builds c-tagged background spectra using: - Pythia dijet flavour-pair fractions binned in (p_T, p_T), validated against ATLAS 7 TeV flavour-fraction data with a small +0.0073 bℓ correction. - GN2 c-tag efficiencies, interpolated log-linearly in p_T between the two published p_T samples. - A multi-hadron efficiency enhancement with α = 1.96, fitted to the observed b-tagged spectra.

Exclusive 1-tag and 2-tag categories are fitted jointly with four-parameter dijet functions. The limit-setting machinery reproduces ATLAS limits to within 10%.

Results: - Z′→cc̄. At ε_c = 0.6, the tagged search beats the inclusive one (gain G > 1) over the whole 2–4 TeV range, with roughly ±10% band dominated by the tagging model. - T→cg. The gain appears only at lower masses. - Doubling the inclusive sensitivity needs ε_ℓ = 0.12 at ε_c = 0.6 (current 0.15, a factor 1.3). At ε_c = 0.3 it needs 0.016 (current 0.083, a factor 5). - Perfect-tagger ceilings are G ≈ 8 (Z′) and G ≈ 2.2 (T).

The conclusion for tagger development is to optimise the loose working points.

Two points are of interest for jet physics: - Gluon splitting in the background. The multi-hadron fraction from g→cc̄ rises with p_T and is larger for charm than for bottom, and FSR scale variation moves the charm fraction f_c by +22/−14% at about 1.5 TeV. - IRC-safe flavour. Under IRC-safe interleaved flavour neutralisation (IFN), about 50% of cone-labelled charm jets become light at low mass. At 4–5.4 TeV only 17% stay charm. This reclassifies which jets count as charm but does not change the predicted tagged spectrum.

Caveats: there are no published p_T-differential c-tag efficiencies, so these are extrapolated. The 13 TeV flavour fractions are extrapolated from simulation (7 TeV data only reach p_T < 500 GeV). The analysis uses binary thresholds only.

arXiv:2609.35653 §

Factorization and virtuality evolution of jet functions in heavy-ion collisions

Carlos Lamas et al.

The provided full text is truncated at the start of Sec. III, so only the formalism is covered here; the numerical results come from the abstract only.

For γ+jet production in AA collisions, the authors model the nuclei as Glauber ensembles of uncorrelated nucleons. They derive an impact-parameter-dependent factorized cross section: thickness beam functions, times the on-shell 2→2 partonic cross section, convolved with an in-medium jet function 𝒥_k(p_I; p_J, X). The jet function explicitly retains the initial virtuality m_I² = p_I², defined in a physical gauge, with quark and gluon projectors fixed by Ward identities.

They then set up BDMPS-Z Feynman rules for this object: - dressed propagators as path integrals with Wilson lines; - a harmonic-oscillator dipole approximation; - a soft-gluon emission vertex; - a new single-line color-survival factor, exp(−½∫dt/λ_R), controlled by the light-cone mean free path λ_R.

Per the abstract: - Integrating over m_I² recovers standard BDMPS-Z p_⊥ broadening and the soft medium-induced spectrum. - The ratio of medium formation time τ₀ to jet formation time controls which initial virtualities contribute. Vacuum radiation dominates when the jet forms well before τ₀. - As τ₀ → 0, the mean free path becomes the controlling scale instead.

This matters for pre-equilibrium quenching and small systems, and offers a formalism-independent object for comparing energy-loss models. The authors frame it as a common ground for the spread in q̂ extractions.

Caveats: - Neglected couplings. Medium couplings to initial-state partons are dropped, justified only at lowest order; couplings to the hard vertex are argued to be subleading. - Limited order. The hard process is LO, and the NLO part is soft-gluon only, for quark jets. - Approximations. The calculation uses the eikonal limit for the leading parton and the harmonic-oscillator approximation. - No dynamical evolution. The authors state that the full virtuality evolution of the shower through the medium is not described dynamically.

Monday, September 28, 2026

arXiv:2609.30357 §

Optimizing Cell-Based Negative Weight Mitigation with Optimal Transport

Lauren Hay et al.

This paper replaces the modified-Euclidean metric in Andersen–Maier cell resampling with IRC-safe OT metrics: event-level EMD in (η,φ) with p_T weights, and the spectral EMD (SEMD). A negative-weight seed grows a hypersphere in metric space until the enclosed net weight is positive, then weights are redistributed as w_i → (Σw/Σ|w|)|w_i|.

The test sample is MG5_aMC@NLO+Pythia 8.3 Z+jets at 13 TeV, with 37.6% negative weights and O(10⁵) events. The reweight fraction is set deliberately high at f_RW = 0.75. Findings: - β choice: β = 1 and 0.5 give the least bias. β = 1 was chosen on cost grounds. - Stage: resampling after hadronization beats parton-shower and Born-level resampling. - Metric comparison: EMD ≳ SEMD, both better than the original metric, most visibly in H_T and leading-jet p_T. - ΣMD: the distance to the original sample stays below the sample’s statistical uncertainty (δσ/σ = 0.0127). - Statistical gain: all variants take 1/f_ESS from 16 to 6. The metric therefore controls bias, not the statistical gain.

For jets specifically, the relevant change is that IRC safety comes from the metric itself. The previous jet-clustering step, and its dependence on algorithm and R, is no longer needed. Jets otherwise enter only as anti-k_t R = 0.4 validation observables, so this is not a substructure paper.

Caveats: - This is proceedings-length and the evidence is mostly histogram comparisons. - Only one low-statistics sample is tested. - The ΣMD figure of merit (arXiv:2609.30418) uses the EMD as its ground metric. That may flatter the EMD-based resampler relative to the Euclidean baseline; this is my inference, not addressed by the authors.

arXiv:2609.30775 §

Parnassus for the CLD Detector: A Generative ML Surrogate for Detector Simulation and Reconstruction at the FCC-ee

Umar Sohail Qureshi et al.

Model. A two-stage conditional flow-matching surrogate for CLD_o2_v07 Geant4 + PandoraPFA, trained on Z→qq̄ (u,d) and bb̄ at 91.2 GeV: - An event-level MLP models N, H_T and missing-momentum components. - A cross-attention DiT (~5M parameters) generates the particles, each with species and d₀, z₀ and their significances. - Conditioning includes the truth production vertex, which is what lets it reproduce displaced heavy-flavour tails. - cc̄ and ss̄ are held out as out-of-distribution tests.

Jet-level results. After Durham exclusive 2-jet clustering, Parnassus beats Delphes on every observable and every metric. Examples: - Jet mass: W₁ = 0.247 vs 1.886 for Delphes. - e₂^(β=2): VLC 0.076 vs 0.865. - m_jj: VLC 0.52 vs 58.6.

Flavour tagging. A transformer tagger (b/c/s/q) trained on surrogate output gives: - Accuracy 0.684 vs 0.693 for full simulation. - b-vs-c mistag at 80% efficiency: 1.58% vs 1.00%, a ~60% relative excess. - Delphes overestimates separability (accuracy 0.732; b-vs-c mistag 0.24%). - A full-sim-trained tagger drops only 0.012 in accuracy when applied to Parnassus.

Speed. 1.3 ms/event on an A100 and 39 ms on a CPU core, vs 6.0 s for full simulation and reconstruction.

This is a credible e⁺e⁻ jet-flavour benchmark. It shows directly that Delphes-based tagging projections are optimistic.

Caveats: - Coordinates and substructure definitions (p_T, ΔR girth) are carried over from the CMS setup rather than e⁺e⁻-native variables; the authors acknowledge this. - Hyperparameters are untuned from the CMS configuration. - bb̄ reproduces worse than the held-out flavours. - The HitPF extension is qualitative and shows impact-parameter deviations. - No boosted or multi-prong substructure is tested.

Friday, September 25, 2026

arXiv:2609.29025 §

Impact of soft QCD effects over intrajet azimuthal anisotropies

Jesus Alberto V. Corral et al.

This is a generator-level reinterpretation of the CMS intrajet v₂ measurement (arXiv:2312.17103), using PYTHIA 8.309 with the Monash, CP5 and QCD-scheme CR tunes. The setup is anti-kT R=0.8 jets with pT>550 GeV and |η|<1.6, particles with 0.3<j_T<3 GeV, and a long-range cut |Δη*|>2.

Tune spectra: CP5 gives a softer N_ch spectrum (inclusive ⟨N_ch⟩ 27.3, vs 31.2 for Monash and 30.0 for QCD-CR), with fewer jets at high N_ch.

v₂ agreement with data (χ²/ndof):

Model N_ch < 80 N_ch ≥ 80
QCD-scheme CR 0.32 10.21
Sherpa (from CMS) 1.02 21.93
Monash 2.28 10.20
CP5 4.69 23.55

The high-N_ch excess persists for all tunes. The authors frame QCD-CR and Monash as “consistent at 2.3σ” in the top bin. That framing sits awkwardly with χ²/ndof ≈ 10 over those two points, and should not be read as agreement.

The new ingredient is rotating into the WTA-axis frame: - WTA lowers v₂ at the lowest N_ch for all tunes. - At high N_ch, Monash and CP5 shift away from their E-scheme values, while QCD-CR is the most frame-stable. - The authors tie the Monash shift to ⟨η*⟩ differences (2.08 for E-scheme vs 2.12 for WTA, at N_ch>80).

This argues that part of the high-N_ch “signal” is sensitive to soft-recoil effects on the jet axis, and it motivates a WTA-frame measurement.

Caveats: - No detector simulation. - The WTA predictions have no data to compare against. - The fit uses only three Fourier terms. - Differences between tunes are attributed to colour reconnection, but CP5 also differs in its ISR, FSR, MPI and α_s settings.

arXiv:2609.26899 §

Towards Foundation Models on Hardware Accelerators for Particle Physics

Maya Benyas et al.

The authors distil the 423M-parameter OmniLearned-Large model into Deep Sets students on the standard Pythia+Delphes top-tagging dataset (\(p_T\) 550–650 GeV). The teacher was pretrained on >1B jets across 210 classes, then fine-tuned to AUC 0.9880 and \(1/\epsilon_B\) = 3365 at \(\epsilon_S\) = 30%. The distillation loss uses α = β = 0.5 and T = 4.

Results at 30% signal efficiency:

Change Rejection
Adding one message-passing layer (\(\log m_{ij}\), \(\log\Delta R_{ij}\), \(\log k_{T,ij}\) on the top-64 constituents; 11k → 17k params) 748 → 1980
Distillation vs. hard-label cross-entropy (same student) 1216 → 1980
Distilling from the pretrained teacher vs. a from-scratch teacher of the same architecture 1020 → 1980

Accuracy moves by only ~0.2–0.3 points across these comparisons, so the pretraining benefit sits almost entirely in the ROC tail. The pretrained teacher itself has ~4× better rejection than its from-scratch counterpart, and roughly half of that gain survives into the student. This is the first demonstration that the pretraining benefit, not just the teacher’s capacity, transfers through distillation. The best student reaches 93.98% accuracy versus 94.38% for OmniLearned-Small, with 155× fewer parameters.

Quantization: - Post-training quantization at 8 bits is destructive: 59% accuracy for the message-passing student, and chance level at 4 bits. - Quantization-aware training at 8 bits (Brevitas) recovers 93.95% accuracy and 1787 rejection.

Caveats: - No HLS/FPGA synthesis, latency or resource numbers yet. - The message-passing student costs 14.3M MACs per jet versus 305k for plain Deep Sets. That 47× gap is what will matter against a µs-scale trigger budget, and the paper does not yet address it. - There is a single hyperparameter point, and K = 64 was not optimized. - Only the saturated top-tagging benchmark is tested.

arXiv:2609.25153 §

Search for boosted VBS W^±W^±H production with same-sign leptons and b-jets

ATLAS Collaboration

First ATLAS search for EW \(W^\pm W^\pm Hjj\), targeting the quartic \(WWHH\) coupling via \(\kappa_{WW}\). The physics result is a null: observed (expected) 95% CL exclusion of \(\kappa_{WW}\) outside \([-2.1,4.1]\) (\([-1.9,3.9]\)), statistically limited (data stat impact 0.22 vs total 0.26 on \(\mu\) for the \(\kappa_{WW}=-2\) benchmark). Weaker than the recent CMS inclusive \(VVH\) result (\(\kappa_{2V}\in[0.4,1.6]\)), but a different channel.

For substructure, the interesting content is the analysis architecture. The boosted \(H\to b\bar b\) candidate is a single anti-\(k_t\) \(R=1.0\) jet built from UFOs (Track-CaloClusters), groomed with Soft-Drop + Constituent Subtraction + SoftKiller, \(250<p_T<3000\) GeV, \(m_J>40\) GeV. Tagging uses GN2X, the transformer \(H\to bb\)/\(cc\)/QCD tagger, with the discriminant in Eq. (1) at \(f_{Hcc}=0.02\); the working point is a mass-flat 1.55% multijet mistag, giving ~80% \(H\to bb\) efficiency. Notably, GN2X calibration is a leading systematic — flavor tagging (signal) contributes 0.050 to the \(\mu\) impact, second only to the fake-factor uncertainty (0.084) and well above jet energy/mass scale (0.014). The VBS forward-jet topology is captured by an RNN tagger on small-\(R\) four-momenta (VBF-RNN) rather than cut-based \(m_{jj}/\Delta\eta_{jj}\), retaining ~80% signal at score >0.2, and explicitly excludes small-\(R\) jets within \(\Delta R<1.4\) of the large-\(R\) Higgs candidate. This is a clean demonstration that mass-decorrelated boosted-\(X\to bb\) taggers plus ML VBS tagging are now the default toolkit for rare EW processes; the caveat is that at these yields (9 observed, 12.5 expected background) the boosted approach is entirely statistics-limited and the resolved topology added negligible sensitivity.

arXiv:2609.22899 §

Exact Amplitude Reconstruction in the Real Common-Phase Sector of Small-x Diffractive Energy Flow

Sanskriti Agrawal et al.

This is a formal small-x/EIC tomography paper; the jet connection runs through energy-flow operators and coherent diffractive dijet production rather than jet substructure in the collider-QCD sense. The authors set up an operator-level map from the impact-parameter-dependent dipole amplitude \(T_Y(r,b,\phi)\) (C-even, real, even cosine harmonics \(T_{2n}\)) to the gluon Wigner harmonics \(W_{2n}\) (via a rotationally diagonal Bessel transform, \(\propto\int r\,dr\,J_{2n}(kr)\tilde T_{2n}\)) and to the longitudinal-photon diffractive dijet amplitude harmonics \(A_{2n}\propto\int\!\int \psi_L T_{2n} J_{2n}(Pr)J_{2n}(\Delta b)\). Their central claim: in the Born leading-eikonal, longitudinal, C-even sector all harmonics share a common overall phase, so \(A_{2n}/A_0\in\mathbb{R}\), and in a node-free bin the normalized amplitude is recovered exactly as \(\sqrt{p_E(\theta)}\), with harmonic ratios from a direct Fourier projection (Eq. 25). For generic complex amplitudes the energy-flow intensity only yields autocorrelations \(s_N=\sum_l a_{l+N}a_l^*\) — the standard phase-retrieval non-uniqueness.

The genuinely new content for energy-flow observables: they treat the diffractive dijet energy-flow distribution as an intensity and give an explicit deconvolution (generating function \(X(t)=\sqrt{1+\mathcal C(t)}\), plus a logarithmic/cumulant representation) that systematically subtracts reducible lower-harmonic mixing — e.g. clarifying that the measured \(\cos4\phi\) moment contains \(2x_2+x_1^2\), so the “true” \(A_4\) requires subtracting the elliptic \(x_1^2\). Caveats are stated forthrightly: the common-phase property is not an all-order theorem — nodes leave a sign ambiguity, odderon/C-odd exchange, dispersive real-part corrections with harmonic-dependent slopes \(\lambda_{2n}\), and \(\mathcal O(\alpha_s)\) radiation all break it. No numerical EIC projections are given; this is a formalism/feasibility paper.

arXiv:2609.24334 §

Multitask Jet Analysis with Vision-Language Models: A Physics-Informed Four-Panel Representation

Lu Zhang et al.

Squarely on jet tagging/substructure, but the novelty is representational rather than physics-reach. Each JetClass jet is rendered as a \(224\times224\) RGB image of four aligned \((\Delta\eta,\Delta\phi)\) panels encoding: (P1) \(p_T\) flow all/charged/neutral; (P2) charged-hadron/neutral-hadron/EM composition; (P3) \(p_T\)-weighted transverse and longitudinal impact-parameter significances plus displaced-track multiplicity (\(s\ge2\)); (P4) signed-track \(p_T\) densities and local \(p_T^{\kappa}\)-weighted (\(\kappa=0.5\)) jet-charge asymmetry. Jets are canonicalized (centroid, PCA rotation for \(a_{\rm PCA}\ge0.05\), deterministic reflections), Gaussian-deposited (\(\sigma=0.03\)) and log-compressed. Four open VLMs are LoRA-adapted for three tasks (10-class tagging, 6-field attribute prediction, panel-consistency/localization).

Results: zero-shot is at chance (3.33–10.57% macro recall); after adaptation on 200k jets (0.2% of JetClass) Gemma4-E4B hits 75.05% macro recall / 74.87% \(F_1\), 93.31% Task-2 field-mean, 99.88%/99.75% consistency/localization. Panel ablations are the physics-relevant payoff: relative to \(p_T\)-flow alone (57.50%), adding species→61.29%, then displacement→67.47% (the dominant handle, via \(b/c\) lifetime), while jet-charge (P4) specifically lifts the near-degenerate hadronic \(Z\to q\bar q\) (Zqq 22.5→34.2%). Fixed-budget WZ reallocation buys up to +9.83 pts on Zqq at ~constant macro recall. Transfer to JetClass-II and real-data Aspen Open Jets holds (>70% Task 1, >92% Task 2/3 with 3k target jets). Caveat, stated by the authors: this is not competitive with ParticleTransformer/ParticleNet on discrimination — 75% 10-class recall is well below dedicated taggers — and the appeal is a single instruction-conditioned multitask interface, not raw performance. Pixelization also discards constituent-level information by construction.

arXiv:2609.16276 §

PSICHE: Probabilistic, Structure-Intrinsic Clustering with a Hierarchical Embedding

Margaret Lazarovits et al.

This introduces a jet clustering algorithm that abandons fixed cone size and fixed multiplicity, instead learning both jointly. The core is a variational Gaussian mixture model (vGMM) embedded inside Bayesian Hierarchical Clustering: BHC handles jet-level associations via tree-consistent merges (restricted to Voronoi/Delaunay nearest neighbors, giving N ln N scaling as in FastJet), while each embedded vGMM models subcluster substructure. Key design choices for substructure: energy-proportional data weights ωₙ = ω·Eₙ preserve four-vector sums (Eqs. 10–11) and give IRC safety at jet level; a subcluster-inheritance + L2-overlap-based merge scheme regulates subcluster multiplicity and provides “IRC protection” at subcluster level (tested by injecting ghost subclusters, none survive). The transfer factor ω = 4 GeV acts as a soft lower resolution bound rather than a hard cone. Notably, PSICHE clusters in space and time (η, φ, t), natively ingesting per-cell measurement precisions Λ*ₙ matched to simulated energy/timing resolutions (σ_t ≈ 100 ps beam spread, CMS-ECAL-like geometry).

Concrete results: on Pythia 8.3 dijets it reproduces AK4/8/15 multiplicity and jet mass/energy/time spectra; on tt̄ it dynamically shrinks jet size ∝ m_jet/p_T, recovering a W/top admixture that no single AK radius captures. On single boosted W it consistently finds two-subcluster structure where a vGMM seeded on AK constituents fails to regulate multiplicity.

For this topic, the significance is a fully unsupervised, probabilistic framework that unifies clustering and substructure resolution with timing and experimental uncertainties — a genuinely different paradigm from sequential recombination + grooming. Caveats: the Λ* addition breaks inverse-Wishart conjugacy (handled approximately); the subcluster IRC argument is empirical, not proven; benchmarks are idealized single-detector simulation, and the pileup-mitigation demonstration is only sketched (text truncated). No timing/throughput numbers versus FastJet are given.

arXiv:2609.18102 §

Cat's Cradle diagrams for substructure studies

Peter Skands

Skands proposes a bookkeeping/visualization framework (“cat’s cradle diagrams”) plotting the parametric size αₛ^A L^λ of each perturbative coefficient versus a p_T-like substructure resolution scale, for a back-to-back qq̄ dipole at √s = 200 GeV (α_s = 0.11). The physics point: in the “resolved jet substructure” region (roughly 5 ≲ p_T ≲ 20 GeV here), neither fixed-order (N^nLO) nor soft-collinear (N^nLL) power counting gives systematic convergence — each delivers only ~half an effective order per full order (Eqs. 9–10 define R_acc and n_eff = log_{αₛ} R_acc). He introduces an intermediate counting N^nLJ, assigning terms with equal 3A−2λ the same order, nested as N^nLO ⊂ N^nLJ ⊂ N^{n+1}LL, with 1/3-order increments.

The concrete claim for substructure: conventional LL p_T-ordered dipole/antenna showers (Pythia, Vincia defaults) actually include the αₛL term beyond pure LL (Eq. 12/15), making them “LJ+LL” accurate — about an order of magnitude better than pure LL resummation across the resolved-substructure region, with a broad plateau of constant n_eff. Azimuthal averaging further compensates the local-recoil αₛ²L² violation by a factor ~1/3 (2/3 vs 1/3 random-walk argument), keeping angular-averaged observables near NLL down to a few GeV. Matrix-element corrections extend this to NLO+LJ+LL, flat over the whole range.

For this topic it offers a quantitative language for what “substructure accuracy” means midway between hard and soft regimes — useful for judging shower/grooming accuracy claims. Appendix A gives exact Z→qgq̄ Ariadne-p_T Sudakov integrals and shows running-coupling coefficients stay O(1) with ~20% per-order growth. Caveats the author states plainly: exponent-level only (not cross-section), one-prong observable tuned to the shower ordering variable, order-unity coefficient assumption tested only at lowest order, final-state qq̄ dipole only, and he explicitly claims no new physics results — the diagrams and N^nLJ counting are the deliverable, and N^nLJ is close to existing double-log counting.

arXiv:2609.17812 §

The production of bottom-flavoured jets at the LHC through bottom-quark pair production at NNLO+PS

Rhorry Gauld et al.

Watchlist hit (jet algorithms, flavour tagging) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

We consider the dominant production mechanism of bottom-flavoured jets at the LHC, which proceeds through bottom-quark pair production. We compute next-to-next-to- leading-order (NNLO) QCD corrections retaining full bottom-quark mass effects and match them with a parton shower (NNLO+PS). Our predictions provide a realistic description of events with one and two bottom-flavoured jets at both the partonic and hadronic level. We compare different jet-flavour definitions and confirm that the dominant numerical differences between the jet algorithms result from the treatment of jets that contain two bottom quarks originating from \(g\to b\bar b\) splittings. We compare our predictions, using the experimental bottom-flavour tagging prescription, with measurements by ATLAS, CMS, and LHCb at 7 and 13 TeV, finding good agreement for the distributions presented by ATLAS and CMS and a good description of the shapes of the LHCb data. We also discuss the impact of various physics effects on the considered distributions: power corrections in the bottom-quark mass, the implications of symmetric/asymmetric jet-selection cuts, as well as contributions from multi-parton interactions.

arXiv:2609.18693 §

Baryon-to-meson ratios in the Lund jet plane: resolving string-junction and thermal-fragmentation effects

Robert Vertesi

Watchlist hit (Lund plane, Lund jet plane) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

Baryon-to-meson ratios inside charged jets are studied in the Lund jet plane using PYTHIA simulations of pp collisions at \(\sqrt{s} = 14\) TeV, with 15 < \(p_{\rm T}^{\rm ch,jet}\) < 30 GeV/\(c\) in an acceptance compatible with the future ALICE 3 detector. Jets are reclustered with the Cambridge–Aachen algorithm and identified hadrons are tagged at the splitting where they first separate from the leading branch. Three model configurations are compared: Monash, color reconnection beyond leading color (CR-BLC), and CR-BLC combined with thermodynamical string fragmentation (Thermal+CR-BLC). The \(\Lambda_c^+/{\rm D}^0\) ratio rises with \(\log(1/z)\) in the two configurations that include color-string junctions and is approximately flat under Monash. The same trend is present at the first Cambridge–Aachen branching, indicating that the effect is set at the level of the color topology rather than through the cumulative action of many soft splittings. Light- and strange-sector ratios show no \(z\)-dependent signature of either mechanism, but the angular projection reveals non-trivial structure at the first branching that is washed out when emissions are summed. The Cambridge–Aachen declustering and the Lund plane together separate the kinematic signatures of the two baryon-enhancement mechanisms in PYTHIA and provide observables accessible to future ALICE 3 measurements.

From Nuclear Many-Body Correlations to Energy Detector Correlators

Jo\~{a}o Barata et al.

Watchlist hit (energy flow) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

Relativistic nuclear collisions have opened an experimental arena for studying many-body correlations in nuclear ground states. However, the connection between initial-state correlations and final-state multi-particle observables measured at colliders is not yet formulated as a systematically improvable matching problem. We show that detector correlators built from asymptotic energy flows provide a natural framework for realizing such a construction. In particular, we express the asymptotic energy flow as a functional of the early-time stress tensor, and expand it in suitable modes to recover the familiar linear hydrodynamic relations at leading order. Then, motivated by small-\(x\) QCD, we map angular projections of detector correlators to multipole-operator correlators computed in the colliding nuclei. We thus establish a systematic formalism for matching long-wavelength correlations between incoming and outgoing QCD states in high-energy hadronic collisions.

PSICHE: Probabilistic, Structure-Intrinsic Clustering with Hierarchical Embedding for Jet Reconstruction

Margaret Lazarovits et al.

A genuinely new clustering paradigm rather than a substructure measurement. PSICHE embeds a variational Gaussian mixture model (vGMM) inside Bayesian Hierarchical Clustering, so that jet multiplicity/scale (tree level) and internal substructure (mixture level) are inferred jointly and unsupervised, with no pre-set cone size or prong count. Key novelties for substructure: (i) dynamically learned, variable jet sizes; (ii) simultaneous multi-scale learning of subclusters; (iii) clustering in space and time (η,φ,t), with per-cell energy/time measurement precisions Λ*_n folded directly into the likelihood; (iv) energy-proportional data weights ω_n=ω·E_n that give jet-level IRC safety and preserve four-vector sums. Subcluster proliferation is controlled by hierarchical inheritance (child multiplicity seeded to sum of parents) plus explicit pairwise merge comparisons via L2 Gaussian overlap. Nearest-neighbor (Voronoi/Delaunay, FastJet 3.4-based) merge restriction brings scaling to ~N ln N.

Concrete substructure results: on boosted single-W samples PSICHE consistently resolves the two-prong parton structure as two subclusters, where a vGMM run post-hoc on AK-jet constituents fails to regulate multiplicity. In tt̄, it dynamically reconstructs both resolved and boosted top/W jets, tracking jet size ∝ m_jet/p_T across p̂_T^min=200→500 GeV, whereas AK jets impose hard cone cutoffs. A top-jet decomposes into three localized prongs (bb + W→qq) plus a dispersive soft-radiation component. The hyperparameter ω (=4 GeV here) softly sets the resolved scale — this replaces the cone parameter but is not eliminated. Validation is against anti-k_T (R=0.4/0.8/1.5) on Pythia 8.3 + idealized CMS-ECAL-like calo/PF simulation; there is no comparison yet to standard substructure taggers (N-subjettiness, soft drop) or performance under realistic detector/BIB effects. The time dimension enabling new time-based substructure observables (subcluster times, space-time covariance) is the most distinctive extension for the field.

arXiv:2609.16114 §

Generalized Detectors at Colliders

Mark Gonzalez et al.

Watchlist hit (energy correlators, jet functions, infrared and collinear safe) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

Recent progress in collider physics has reformulated phenomenological questions in terms of detector correlation functions and advanced their theoretical understanding. These advances have primarily focused on correlators of the average null energy operator, \(\mathcal{E}(\vec{n})\), known as energy correlators. However, colliders have access to a much broader class of detector operators, \(\mathcal{E}^n_R(\vec{n})\), which measure powers of the energy on a subset of hadrons \(R\), such as charged hadrons. These generalized detectors are generically not infrared and collinear safe, and their description requires nonperturbative matching between the hadronic detectors measured in the infrared and the partonic detectors used in ultraviolet calculations. We develop a framework for computing their multi-point correlation functions. We introduce universal nonperturbative matching coefficients, termed “detector functions”, that implement this infrared-ultraviolet matching. For the \(\mathcal{E}_R^n\) operators studied here, these coefficients are represented by energy-weighted moments of single- and multi-hadron fragmentation functions. We present their renormalization group structure, derive QCD factorization theorems for the projected correlators, and compute jet functions through next-to-leading order. In the fixed-coupling pure Yang-Mills limit, we derive the light-ray OPE of hadronic detectors and connect it to the QCD factorization framework. We also identify universal nonperturbative power corrections generated by soft radiation, which are enhanced in the collinear limit and can substantially modify the perturbative angular scaling. A parton shower study finds qualitative agreement with the predicted perturbative and nonperturbative scaling behaviors. Our work significantly broadens the space of detector operators under theoretical control, with potential phenomenological applications.

arXiv:2609.16342 §

Top-quark pair production sensitivity to heavy new physics at the FCC-ee

Jacopo De Piccoli et al.

Watchlist hit (flavour tagging, jet flavour) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

We study the sensitivity of top-quark pair production at the FCC-ee to heavy new physics, parametrised in the Standard Model effective field theory, using discriminants based on matrix elements. The analysis includes detector reconstruction effects, obtained with a fast simulation of the IDEA detector concept, and jet flavour tagging. We consider dimension-six operators that modify the electroweak couplings of the top quark or generate four-fermion interactions, at leading order and in diagrams with two resonant top quarks. The full six-body kinematic information is employed to maximise the discrimination power against the Standard Model. Two bins per dimension in the space of 12 discriminants, one per operator considered, provide a simple and robust methodology to set nearly optimal multidimensional constraints. Bounds on the new physics scale can reach values up to 30 TeV in a single-operator fit and up to 5 TeV in a global multi-operator fit. A combination with High-Luminosity LHC prospects on top-quark operators shows complementary sensitivities which help lift approximate degeneracies.

arXiv:2609.13366 §

Flavor Flux Correlators, Forward-Backward Asymmetries, and Anomalies

Kyle Lee et al.

Watchlist hit (energy correlators) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

Flavor plays a crucial role in the structure of the Standard Model. As such, many important collider physics measurements incorporate the flavor information of detected hadrons. Recent progress in the study of energy correlators has enabled collider physics measurements of energy flux to be reformulated as operator statements. In this Letter, we introduce flavor flux correlators, which measure the angular distribution and correlations of flavor quantum numbers. We emphasize a unique feature of heavy flavor flux correlators as compared to correlators of other charges, namely the \(b\) and \(c\) flavor quantum numbers are gapped at the heavy quark mass scale, making them a particularly clean perturbative observables, with \(\mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{\text{QCD}}/m_Q)\) suppressed non-perturbative power corrections. As an application, we reformulate the heavy quark forward-backward asymmetry as a macroscopic consequence of a mixed electroweak anomaly of the heavy-quark flavor current. Using the relation to the anomaly, we compute the flavor flux correlator analytically at next-to-next-to-next-to-leading order and verify using parton shower simulations that non-perturbative corrections are suppressed, as expected from the gapped nature of the heavy flavor quantum numbers. Flavor flux correlators can be measured using archival electron-positron collision data, as well as at Belle II and future colliders.

arXiv:2609.12024 §

Learning the Geometry of Collider Events with Metric-Aware Deep Sets

Lauren Hay et al.

Watchlist hit (particle flow) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

Optimal transport gives structured data a geometry, but exact evaluation is costly in large pairwise analyses that exploit relationships among distances. Learned surrogates are faster, but need not preserve this metric structure. We develop a Deep Sets surrogate for OT between variable-size weighted point clouds that enforces non-negativity, exchange symmetry, and zero self-distance, leaving the triangle inequality unconstrained. Applied to the Energy Mover’s Distance between collider events in a particle physics application, the Metric-Aware Particle Flow Network achieves percent-level mean absolute percentage error while significantly improving inference throughput over other exact and approximate methods surveyed. The architectural constraints are found to improve properties that are not explicitly enforced: across \(10^6\) held-out event triplets, triangle-inequality violations fall from 199 for a matched unconstrained network to 2, and the maximum from 149.5 to 5.8 GeV. These results demonstrate that targeted inductive biases can yield fast neural surrogates with substantially improved geometric fidelity.

arXiv:2609.12064 §

One-point charge correlator as a probe for the odderon

Haotian Cao et al.

Watchlist hit (energy correlator, infrared and collinear safe) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

We propose the one-point charge correlator (OPCC) in transversely polarized deep inelastic scattering as a new probe of the spin-dependent odderon. The OPCC is an infrared and collinear safe observable constructed solely from the charge and angular information of final-state charged particles. Because the charge weight is odd under charge conjugation, the contribution of the C-even pomeron to the OPCC vanishes identically in the small-\(x\) eikonal limit, whereas the contribution of the C-odd spin-dependent odderon survives. We define a transverse single-spin asymmetry by normalizing the spin-dependent OPCC to the unpolarized charged-hadron one-point energy correlator. At small \(x\), this asymmetry reduces to a ratio of spin-dependent odderon and pomeron contributions. We provide illustrative estimates of this asymmetry for Electron-Ion Collider kinematics within the small-\(x\) dipole framework with Balitsky–Kovchegov evolution to guide experimental studies. Dedicated measurements of the OPCC asymmetry with a transversely polarized proton beam would provide new quantitative constraints on the currently unconstrained spin-dependent odderon.

arXiv:2609.10679 §

Computationally Efficient Description of Medium Response to Jets in Heavy Ion Collisions

Jorge Casalderrey-Solana et al.

Only tangential to jet substructure per se: the paper computes the hydrodynamic wake (medium response) that contaminates the soft component of reconstructed jets in heavy-ion collisions, not substructure observables directly. Its relevance is that this soft contribution cannot be groomed away for most observables, so a correct wake description is a prerequisite for computing jet/substructure observables in AA. The concrete advance is an “Efficient Wake” procedure: precompute a library of 34 template (Green’s-function) solutions to (1+1)D linearized hydro on a Bjorken background for unit energy-momentum deposition (deposition times τ_temp^d = kΔτ, Δτ=0.4 fm/c, τ₀=0.4 to τ_f=13.6 fm/c, T_f=145 MeV ⇒ T₀=446 MeV), then reconstruct wakes in a radially expanding droplet by superposing templates with suitable rotations/boosts to fold in transverse radial flow at freezeout.

Benchmarked against full nonlinear (3+1)D MUSIC hydro for 50 representative partons at two energies, they find reasonable agreement and — the headline — a speedup of “at least tens of thousands” up to a factor 200,000, while giving a much better p_T spectrum (harder, matching data’s demand for more particles above ~2 GeV) than the constant+cosine Hybrid-Model treatment. Caveats stated: the approximations are not controlled by smallness of the perturbation (validity assessed only empirically via the 50-event comparison), and implementation in a jet Monte Carlo (where 10⁵–10⁶ wakes per analysis are needed) remains future work.

arXiv:2609.11692 §

Hunting the Unseen: Deep Learning Analysis for Semi-Visible Jet Tagging

Miguel A. Avenda\~no-Bernal et al.

Squarely a jet-substructure paper. It benchmarks five DL classifiers for SVJ (Z′→dark quarks, Hidden Valley) vs QCD dijet discrimination, combining global kinematics (MET, Δφ(MET,jet), M_jj, M_T) with substructure: primary Lund Jet Plane, two-point EEC e₂^(β=1), angularity τ_{α,1} for α=0.1,0.8,1.5, and charged-hadron multiplicity. Architectures: a ViT on 50×50 LJP images (patchified 10×10, 6 encoder blocks, 4 heads), JetLOV (RegNet learning node features on the C/A tree, feeding LundNet EdgeConv), an MLP on 15 high-level observables, and two fusion networks (ViT+MLP, JetLOV+MLP). Training on 300k+300k events across 18 BPs (m_Z′=2.5, 4.5 TeV; scans of m_ψd, r_inv, Λ_dark), with three held-out 3.5 TeV BPs to test interpolation across parameter space.

The key benchmark result for the field: global kinematic observables outperform both jet-representation networks individually; among substructure representations the hierarchical clustering-tree (JetLOV) beats LJP images; and fusing structured representations with global kinematics gives the best overall performance. This quantifies how much discrimination lives in radiation-pattern structure beyond MET/global variables — modest but complementary. Caveats visible in text: no pileup in the samples (angularity especially would need mitigation), Pythia default hadronisation parameters held fixed, and r_inv is not an explicit Pythia parameter but tuned via dark-hadron branching fractions. Full AUC numbers are in tables not reproduced in the provided text.

arXiv:2609.10716 §

Soft functions for generalised angularity event shapes at NNLO

Emmet P. Byrne et al.

Watchlist hit (event shape, event shapes) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

We consider a class of dijet event shapes which tend to angularity in the forward limit. The factorisation formula for this class of event shapes differs from angularity only in the soft function. The NNLO soft function of an event shape in this class is therefore the only missing ingredient required for NNLL’ resummation. We describe a modular approach to the calculation of the soft function for any event shape in this class, at NLO and NNLO. Much of the structure of the soft function can be written in terms of a single rapidity integral. The remainder can be computed as a three-dimensional numerical integral for which we provide a code. To demonstrate the flexibility of our approach, we compute the NNLO soft function for three new families of event shapes in this class. Potential applications include the investigation of hadronisation effects at future \(e^+ e^-\) colliders, or through the re-analysis of archived data.

arXiv:2609.11604 §

Collinear fragmentation of pseudoscalar quarkonia from NLO NRQCD

Francesco Giovanni Celiberto et al.

Watchlist hit (jet fragmentation) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

We discuss a new family of publicly available collinear fragmentation functions (FFs) for pseudoscalar quarkonia, the NRFF1.0 set. It builds upon the Heavy-Flavor Non-Relativistic evolution (HF-NRevo) scheme, designed to describe heavy-hadron formation through leading-power fragmentation at moderate and large transverse momentum. Heavy-quarkonium production naturally involves both perturbative and non-perturbative QCD dynamics, from the production of the heavy \(Q\bar{Q}\) pair to its formation into a physical bound state. Within NRFF1.0, Non-Relativistic Quantum Chromodynamics (NRQCD) provides the theoretical framework for calculating the FF inputs at the initial scale, which are subsequently evolved through the HF-NRevo scheme. This setup provides a precision baseline for investigating the underlying partonic hierarchy and jet structure across the moderate- to high-transverse-momentum regime. The explicit treatment of partonic channels and heavy-flavor thresholds makes this framework particularly suitable for exploring quarkonium-in-jet fragmentation, jet-quenching sensitivity, energy-loss mechanisms, and the emergence of medium-modified fragmentation patterns in the quark-gluon plasma.

arXiv:2609.09275 §

"Transforming" LHCb: self-supervised maps of heavy-flavour decays

Marko Stamenkovic et al.

Watchlist hit (flavour tagging, jet flavour) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

Decays of beauty and charm hadrons provide sensitive probes of physics beyond the standard model, including decays with invisible particles, in which part of the final state leaves no reconstructed detector signature. The large heavy-flavour data samples recorded by the LHCb experiment at the CERN LHC, together with its precise tracking, displaced vertex reconstruction, and particle identification, make it particularly well suited to learning a map of reconstructed heavy-hadron decay environments directly from data. We propose to bring recent advances in jet flavour tagging at ATLAS and CMS to significantly improve on the performance of the current LHCb taggers and extend them to the reconstruction of heavy-flavour decays with several invisible particles in the final state. To achieve this, we introduce a self-supervised transformer architecture that learns the decay maps without flavour or exclusive-decay labels by inferring masked particle identification information and completing jets from which constituents have been removed. Across five classification tasks in simulated LHCb Open Data, the self-supervised model outperforms an otherwise identical transformer with random weights, and performs comparably to a fully supervised transformer. We achieve a tagging power of about 10\%. In addition, removing constituents from reconstructed exclusive decays also systematically increases the model anomaly score relative to random removals from the same heavy hadrons. We confirm this behaviour directly in 2017 LHCb proton-proton collision Open Data: the score increases for all eight studied heavy-flavour channels, and the signal region response exceeds that in the adjacent sidebands. These studies provide a proof of principle that mapping heavy-flavour decay environments through jets can transform flavour tagging in LHCb and extend the discovery reach for incomplete or otherwise unusual decays.

arXiv:2609.06686 §

Likelihood-Based Unsupervised Anomaly Detection in CMS Dijet Events (Bhavishya Chebrolu

VIT-AP University et al.

Trains a rational-quadratic neural spline flow (10 blocks, 256 hidden units, 8 bins) on 61 high-level dijet/event observables from CMS 2011B HT AOD open data (√s=7 TeV, 378,278 events, ~0.5 fb⁻¹), scoring events by −log p(x) with no signal model, MC, or sidebands. Selecting the 99.9th percentile (379 events) and running KS tests across all 61 features, the largest population-level deviations are overwhelmingly jet-substructure variables: leading-jet girth (D=0.575), τ₁/τ₂/τ₃ (0.548/0.541/0.518), the ECF ratio (0.508), τ₃₂ (0.498), τ₂₁ (0.441), width (0.386), then jet mass (0.373) and HT (0.343). The anomalous events show no m_jj resonance — a sliding-window bump hunt gives a look-elsewhere-corrected global p=6.0×10⁻⁴ (test stat 8.79), which they explicitly decline to call evidence.

For jet substructure the relevance is real but the framing is honest: substructure observables emerge as the dominant discriminants of the “non-QCD-like” tail, and the paper gives a useful discussion of why ECFs (recoil-free, subjet-free, IRC-safe) and N-subjettiness give coherent signals. Important self-stated caveats: the flow is trained and scored in-sample (no held-out split, acknowledged likelihood-scale bias); selecting the tail of a multivariate density trivially sculpts the marginals, so KS p-values (e.g. 10⁻¹¹⁹ for girth) are diagnostic only, not significances; and the τ₂₁/τ₃₂=0 spikes are known PF low-multiplicity artifacts. Methodological/exploratory contribution rather than a substructure measurement per se.

The Colors of Jet Quenching

Hannah Bossi et al.

Watchlist hit (energy correlators) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

We combine inclusive jet nuclear modification factors with energy-energy correlators to perform a data-driven extraction of the quark and gluon quenching factors and the medium resolution scale from the small-angle region. Taking coherent energy loss as a null hypothesis, we find jet quenching factors incompatible with Casimir scaling. Incorporating color decoherence through antenna energy loss improves the description of the data and restores Casimir scaling, providing evidence that the quark-gluon plasma partially resolves the internal structure of jets in heavy-ion collisions.

arXiv:2609.05614 §

Energy-energy correlators and color decoherence from a generating functional

Yacine Mehtar-Tani

Watchlist hit (energy correlators) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

Using jet calculus at leading-logarithmic accuracy, we develop a generating-functional approach to describe the hard-collinear sector of jets propagating through the quark-gluon plasma. This framework yields coupled evolution equations for the jet nuclear modification factor and energy-energy correlators (EECs). We first construct the EEC evolution in the fully incoherent regime, and then derive evolution equations that account for the transition from the coherent to the incoherent limit in the large-\(N_c\) limit. Furthermore, we show that combining the EEC with the jet nuclear modification factor provides complementary sensitivity to the overall jet suppression and to medium-induced modifications of the jet’s internal structure. This framework therefore offers a way to constrain the magnitude and flavor dependence of jet energy loss, together with the medium resolution angle governing color decoherence.

arXiv:2609.04306 §

Hadronization effects and electroweak contributions in DIS one-jettiness

Radja Boughezal et al.

Watchlist hit (event shapes) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

We study the structure of leading hadronization effects and the contributions of massive electroweak gauge boson exchange in the \(\tau_1\) and \(\tau_{1a}\) 1-Jettiness global event shapes for deep inelastic scattering (DIS). The leading hadronization effects in \(\tau_1\) acquire a non-trivial dependence on the hard scattering kinematics. This kinematic dependence is explicitly calculable, so that the leading hadronization effects in \(\tau_1\), \(\tau_{1a}\), and DIS thrust are universal and described by the same underlying shape function. The additional calculable kinematic dependence in \(\tau_1\) provides an independent lever arm for simultaneously constraining hadronization effects in these observables. We present corresponding results at the N\(^3\)LL+\({\cal O}(\alpha_s^2)\) level of accuracy. We extend previous results by including the contributions mediated by the exchange of the massive \(Z\) and \(W\) electroweak gauge bosons for neutral current (NC) and charged current (CC) DIS, respectively, including \({\cal O}(\alpha_s)\) QCD corrections to obtain N\(^2\)LL+\({\cal O}(\alpha_s)\) results. We compare theoretical predictions to Pythia simulation and demonstrate the universality of leading hadronization effects across NC and CC DIS processes and a wide range of kinematics relevant to HERA and the EIC.

Quantum Graph Neural Networks for Jet Tagging on Quantum Hardware

Benjamin Jobilal et al.

Watchlist hit (jet charge, particle flow, jet tagging, flavor tagging) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

Jets are central to the physics programs of both current and future colliders, from precision Standard Model measurements and searches for new physics at the Large Hadron Collider to studies of nucleon structure at the future Electron-Ion Collider. Motivated by these applications, we explore quantum machine learning for jet classification and present a permutation-invariant Quantum Graph Neural Network (QGNN) applied to particle-cloud representations of jets. We apply the model to two such discrimination tasks: quark vs. gluon and up vs. down quark flavor tagging, with the latter being, to our knowledge, the first application of a quantum model to this problem. Tracing Gluon Saturation through Hadronization at EIC

Weiyao Ke et al.

Watchlist hit (energy correlator) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

Gluon saturation provides a window into the nonlinear nature of the strong interaction in nuclear matter. One direct consequence of saturation is the \(\boldsymbol{k}_T\) broadening in the final state.We investigate how hadronization reshapes conventional signatures of gluon saturation at EIC within a complete event-generator framework. arXiv:2609.03072 §

Energy-Energy Correlators in Z-Tagged Jets in pp Collisions

Jo\~{a}o Barata et al.

This is squarely a jet-substructure paper and a clean methodological advance. They compute the collinear EEC inside forward Z-tagged jets by grafting an exclusive differential EEC jet function 𝒢_c^EEC onto the back-to-back Z+jet TMD factorization (replacing the unmeasured exclusive jet function, leaving TMD PDFs, hard, and soft functions intact). The EEC jet function is built from the inclusive one via one-loop anti-k_T matching coefficients, evolved with NLO timelike splitting functions from a b_χ-dependent boundary condition to μ_J, and the whole thing is run at NLL′ with a variable-flavor-number scheme. The key physics lever is retaining separate quark and gluon non-perturbative scales, a_q = 2.31 GeV (from e⁺e⁻ global fit, quark-dominated) and a_g = 3.8 GeV (from CMS inclusive-jet EEC, gluon-dominated).

The concrete result: predictions for LHCb-like forward kinematics (2.5<η_J<4.0, R=0.5) at √s=8 and 13 TeV in p_JT bins (20,30), (30,50), (50,100) GeV. The EEC shows a finite small-z_χ plateau (from the exponential large-b_χ suppression making the Hankel transform finite as z_χ→0) and, in angular representation, the characteristic confinement turnover, with the quark contribution turning over at smaller ⟨p_JT⟩χ than the gluon (reflecting a_q<a_g). The flavor decomposition shows quark dominance growing with p_JT and with √s. The physics case: because a_q is fixed independently from e⁺e⁻, measuring this quark-enriched sample tests universality of the confinement transition scale across collision environments. Caveat stated plainly: a_g is poorly constrained (extracted at LL with a single flavor-averaged parameter), and the factorization is unreliable for k_χ≥Q_J (z_χ≥0.063 at R=0.5); NP-parameter uncertainties are not included in the bands.

A Task Force on Strong Coupling Determinations from Event Shapes

Anthony Badea et al.

Watchlist hit (event shape, event shapes) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

The strong coupling constant \(\alpha_s\) is a fundamental parameter of the Standard Model. Its precise determination is essential for accurately predicting, studying, and understanding processes at the Large Hadron Collider and future experiments such as the Future Circular Collider. Event shape and correlator observables measured at electron-positron colliders provide one of the cleanest environments for extracting \(\alpha_s\), thanks to their sensitivity to \(\alpha_s\) and the availability of high-precision data from the Large Electron-Positron Collider. More broadly, such observables provide an ideal setting to develop and test our understanding of the perturbative and non-perturbative elements of Quantum Chromodynamics, which will underpin the field’s precision and discovery frontiers for decades to come. Anomaly detection for multijet scenarios

Gregor Kasieczka et al.

Watchlist hit (jet substructure, soft drop) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

Signals of physics beyond the Standard Model continue to resist discovery at the LHC. arXiv:2609.01416 §

Probing jet medium interaction with generalized projected energy correlators

Balbeer Singh

Watchlist hit (energy correlators) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

We study medium modifications to generalized projected energy correlators, referred to as \(\nu\)-correlators, measured on jets in heavy-ion collisions. While the vacuum distributions of these correlators exhibit a characteristic \(1/\nu\) enhancement, we show that in a dense medium this behavior is regulated within both the BDMPS-Z and semi-classical approximations as \(\nu \to 0\). As a result, contributions from medium-induced emissions become parametrically suppressed relative to the vacuum baseline. We observe a similar trend in the simulated events generated with JEWEL. We find that while the large angle enhancement is qualitatively similar across different values of \(\nu\), the small angle distribution changes drastically between \(\nu<1\) and \(\nu>1\). This indicates that small angle modifications, primarily driven by jet energy loss, are imprinted differently across \(\nu\)-correlators. In particular, for \(\nu<1\) the small angle enhancement is less prominent which suggests that these correlators may provide improved sensitivity to medium modifications in the large angle region.

arXiv:2608.29412 §

Evidence for the dead-cone effect in bottom quark initiated jets produced in proton-proton collisions at √(s) = 13 TeV

CMS Collaboration

Watchlist hit (subjets, quark jets) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

The suppression of collinear gluon emissions from a massive quark, or the ``dead-cone effect,’’ is the primary manifestation of quark mass effects in parton showers. A proton-proton collision data set recorded by the CMS experiment at \(\sqrt{s}\) = 13 TeV with an integrated luminosity of 59.8 fb\(^{-1}\) is analyzed. A differential measurement is presented of the emission density of bottom quark jets, studied as a function of the angular separation between iteratively declustered subjets. The measurement is performed in bottom quark initiated jets with transverse momenta between 40 and 200 GeV originating from top quark pair production. Compared to data, a parton shower Monte Carlo model that includes the dead-cone effect is favored, while a model without the dead-cone effect is disfavored with a significance of 4.3 standard deviations.

arXiv:2608.28089 §

Next generation tracking and vertexing detectors

Armin Ilg

Watchlist hit (pile-up mitigation) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

Precise and efficient track and vertex reconstruction is essential to exploit the physics potential of collider experiments. The requirements of future tracking systems are determined by the collider type and its collision environment, required measurement precision, and beam structure. This contribution reviews the requirements and challenges of vertexing and tracking detectors at future \(e^+e^-\), \(e^-\)-hadron, \(\mu^+\mu^-\), and hadron colliders. Several common trends emerge across the different collider types. Tracking systems require increasingly precise spatial measurements, low-material designs, and, in some cases, integrated particle identification capabilities. Monolithic active pixel sensors are currently the leading option for vertex detectors at \(e^+e^-\) and \(e^-\)-hadron colliders. Tracker concepts range from gaseous detectors, which provide many measurements per track and can achieve very low material budgets, to silicon and scintillating-fibre trackers, which provide fewer but more precise measurements and can tolerate higher hit rates. At 10 TeV parton centre-of-mass colliders, precision timing throughout the tracking system is needed for beam-background rejection or pile-up mitigation. For future hadron colliders specifically, radiation tolerance beyond that demonstrated by existing sensor technologies is required. Ultimately, meeting the vertexing and tracking requirements of future colliders will require substantial R&D and the integrated optimisation of sensors, front-end electronics, readout, cooling, powering, and mechanics.

arXiv:2608.26246 §

Positivity in energy correlators and the event distribution formula

Alexandre Belin et al.

Watchlist hit (energy correlators) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

Energy correlators are universal observables, well defined across a wide range of theories and spacetime dimensions, from gauge theory and conformal field theory to string theory. Energy correlators are constrained by three fundamental properties: pointwise positivity of the energy flux, global positivity originating from their interpretation as state norms in a unitary theory, and energy conservation organizing multi-point energy correlators into an infinite consistent hierarchy. We argue that the most general solution to the infinite hierarchy positivity problem of energy correlators is given by the event distribution formula, which expresses energy correlators as moments of the measure on the space of probability measures on the celestial sphere. We work out in detail implications of positivity for two- and three-point energy correlators and show that it implies nontrivial two-sided bounds on their multipole expansion coefficients. The bounds obtained by requiring consistency of the infinite hierarchy of energy correlators are strictly stronger than those obtained by imposing positivity of the two- and three-point correlators alone. For the low-spin multipole coefficients studied in the paper, the derived bounds are optimal in the sense that their extrema are realized by finite mixtures of finite-particle events. We further demonstrate consequences of positivity in energy correlators in collider physics and conformal field theories.

arXiv:2608.25983 §

Jet Quenching Meets Gluon Saturation

Paul Caucal et al.

Watchlist hit (jet function, jet fragmentation) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

We present a theoretical framework for jet fragmentation in heavy-ion collisions based on the resummation of large energy logarithms. Exploiting the hierarchy of scales characteristic of jet quenching, we show that the jet function obeys the Banfi-Marchesini-Smye evolution equation, with medium-induced energy loss and color decoherence encoded in the initial condition. This structure reveals a close correspondence with saturation physics. In particular, the coherence angle emerges as the analog of the saturation scale and exhibits the same asymptotic scaling behavior under nonlinear evolution. As a proof of principle, we compute the jet nuclear modification factor to quantify the interplay between vacuum radiation and medium-induced color decoherence. Our framework provides a unified perturbative description of vacuum-like parton showers, medium-induced radiation, and color-coherence effects, paving the way for precision studies of jet quenching at RHIC and the LHC.

arXiv:2608.21982 §

Down-Type Jet Identification in Fully Hadronic tt̅ Events

Jinhui Guo et al.

Watchlist hit (jet identification) — abstract only, not yet read for a briefing.

Fully hadronic \(t\bar t\) events carry the largest branching fraction and provide the down-type quark as a near-maximal spin analyzer (\(\beta_d\simeq1\)), yet they are rarely used for spin-correlation and entanglement studies because the down-type jets must be extracted from a difficult jet-to-parton reconstruction in the presence of a large QCD multijet background. Compared with a ST1 only benchmark with random down/up labels, the learned ST2 classifier improves both the effective spin-analysis factor and the purity-weighted six-parton exact fraction at fixed event selection. A direct check of the standard spin-correlation coefficient \(D\) further gives an ML-reconstructed value compatible with the truth-level value within the resampled spread. These results show that down/up identification can retain useful spin-correlation information in the all-hadronic channel.
